Fire rate0.28 /severy 3 s (0.333 /s), the mini's own interval
Range4 cm4 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre (diagonals too).
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 4 cm, beside other towers.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.28 shots/s but the mini fires every 3 s (0.333/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Never attacks: Update runs it every frame and always tells the engine not to aim or fire it.
  • Every 3 s (the mini's fire interval, read directly) it pulses: the blade snaps, a flash runs to each neighbour and one shot is counted. The pulse changes nothing in play.

Targeting

  • No targeting: it ignores the dudes entirely.

Effects

  • Aura (Adjacent, Joe's answer 21): every placed tower on the ring round it (the 6 touching hexes, or the 8 tiles round it on a square table; AuraReach 1.5 cells) gets +30% range.
  • The buff goes into the receiver's AuraRange, which multiplies the reach of its base line and of its card lines.
  • A yellow tape ribbon runs to each tower in reach. It pays out at 2.4 lengths per second and reels in 1.5 times as fast when the neighbour leaves. This is visual only.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Its own line (0 damage) is never read. Cards on it change nothing it does, except support keystones: the engine scales its buff by SupportKeystones.BuffStrength, and SupportKeystoneRules.Reaches can widen or narrow who it reaches.

Quirks

  • It counts toward the engine's cap on how many auras can reach one tower (CardsConfig.AuraCap). A receiver that has hit the cap gets nothing from it.
  • The range gain only helps towers whose code reads api.RangeOf or the mini's range times AuraRange. Towers with a fixed reach in code ignore it (Torch, Third Rail, Toaster Twins, Water Tower, Trebuchet dead zone).
  • A tower with the YOLO! keystone can never be buffed, so it gets nothing.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, AuraFor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: none
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CoverageReport.cs, Tables/GameStore/GameStoreCheck.cs

  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • A hit ends the projectile whatever its Pierce.
  • Bot placement: reach 24 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each throw (and each repeat from cards) rolls its own d6 and lobs a die carrying card damage x the roll (the repeats also x RepeatDamage).
  • A one to five hits one dude with that card damage (Strike): the dude the die strikes in flight, or, when it comes down on the terrain, the nearest dude within 2.1 cm of where it lands (nobody: no hit).
  • Only a six splashes (Joe, #250): a DamageAround of that card damage over the line's Radius (5.5 cm at base), at the shared Splash share (85%) and falloff.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the shot's card damage (Damage stat) as the damage of one pip; Repeats and RepeatDamage give more dice, each with its own roll.
  • Its line carries the Splash tag, so splash cards are offered and the line's Radius stat is the six's burst; SplashesOnlyInBursts keeps the shared 85% to the burst, so a one to five hits whole.
  • All damage goes through Strike (directly, or through DamageAround on a six), so card on-hit effects apply.

Quirks

  • Damage swings from 1 to 6 x per throw, average 3.5 x.
  • A die can hit a dude in flight (it has a hit radius) and then lands where it is, not on the ground.
  • A six hits the dude at the middle for 85% of the roll (the splash's share), so a six at the middle does 5.1 pips, still more than a five.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, OnProjectileHit, OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround, Launch, Strike
  • Shared rules it reads: HitRules.SplashShare
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Tables/GameStore/GameStoreCheck.cs

RULES VS THE BIG ONE

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsPulsewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
KeywordsDirect, DebuffJoe's unit list (#247)
Damage per hit10.036 hp (x DamageScale 1/1000 x Balance.Damage 36)
Fire rate0.182 /severy 5.5 s (0.182 /s), the mini's own interval
Range24 cm24 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Bot placement: reach 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Self-driven: every 5.5 s (divided by its aura rate, x0.95 to 1.05) it makes a ruling on its target once it has aimed at it for 0.35 s.
  • A ruling strips the dude's armour and shield and marks it arguing for 4 s (a boss or non-grunt) or 3.5 s (a grunt).
  • It never strikes: no damage at all. It is non-damaging (Joe, answer 24): it levels from the dudes it rules against, and no damage card is offered to it.

Targeting

  • Keeps its current target while that dude is alive and not arguing, without re-checking range or sight.
  • Otherwise it takes the dude it can see within range with the most hp left (a boss counts 100000 hp more), skipping dudes already arguing.

Effects

  • no-armour and no-shield marks for 4 s or 3.5 s: armour counts for nothing and its shield blocks nothing.
  • A dude marked arguing takes x1.3 from every hit from any tower (DamageTaken).
  • arguing is a decoy mark: the tower earns decoy time for it.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads only the Range stat (api.RangeOf) for choosing a target. Damage cards are never offered (NonDamaging).
  • Damage, fire rate and Repeats are ignored; fire-rate cards do nothing, only the aura rate shortens the cooldown.
  • It never calls Strike, so no card on-hit effect ever happens from it.

Quirks

  • Nothing holds the dude: it keeps walking (issue 439 made it a ruling). Argue (3) and BossArgue (0.8) are overwritten by 3.5 and 4 s and only appear in the log text.
  • Bosses get the longer mark, not a shorter one.
  • Several Lawyers stack: the engine multiplies each DamageTaken, so two Lawyers give x1.69 on a dude marked arguing.
  • It keeps working while the towers' fire is held: it never asks whether it may shoot.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, DamageTaken
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, RangeOf, StripArmour, StripShield
  • Marks it puts on dudes: arguing, no-armour, no-shield
  • Marks it reads: arguing
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CcWall.cs, Tables/GameStore/GameStoreCheck.cs

The Meta Chaser #

T-027 · meta-chaser · gamestore · 120 points

Home
The Game-Store Demo Table (in that table's tray)

T-027, TowerKind 39
Price
120 points
Affinity
Sharp
Role
shooter (its card pool)
Also called
Meta
Tray blurb
No plan of his own. Copies whichever neighbour is winning. Has opinions about last week.
Tray tag line
COPIES A NEIGHBOUR

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsProjectilewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
KeywordsAdjacent, CopycatJoe's unit list (#247)
Damage per hit414.4 hp (x DamageScale 1/10 x Balance.Damage 36)
Fire rate0.6 /severy 1.7 s (0.588 /s), the mini's own interval
Range24 cm24 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre.
  • Bot placement: reach 22 cm, beside other towers.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • With a neighbour to copy, he fires that tower's first attack line from his own spot (through api.Fire, so its own Fire code runs) at 100% of its card damage, with its Repeats and RepeatDamage.
  • His reload when copying is the source's own interval with its cards (api.IntervalOf), without its aura rate, divided by 1, with the usual jitter (Joe, answer 20: 1:1 minus external buffs).
  • With nobody to copy he fires a plain straight bolt of his own line's card damage every mini fire interval (divided by his own aura rate).
  • A ring flashes under the copied tower each shot, and he logs each switch of source.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: he picks the nearest dude he can see, by the sight rules of the tower he copies, with a 0.35 s aim lock; no hunted or boss priority.
  • When copying, his reach is the source's own reach with its cards (api.RangeOf), without its aura range; on the starter bolt it is api.RangeOf of his own line.
  • Source choice (Adjacent, answer 21): of the towers on the ring round him (the 6 touching hexes, or the 8 tiles round him on a square table), the one with the most kills this game, ties to the dearer one; towers that are self-driven, stand on the road, are another Meta Chaser, have no card build or have 0 damage on their first line are skipped (answer 20: attackers only).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • His own cards matter only for the starter bolt (damage and range).
  • A copied shot uses the source's first line as its own cards make it (MetaChaserSpec.OwnLine: worked out with no neighbours), so its cards (damage, on-hit effects, Repeats) apply, at 100% damage; the hit is scaled by the source's DamageScale.
  • The source's range and fire-rate cards count for his reach and reload. What other towers give the source does not (Joe, answer 20, #250): its aura damage, rate and range, a card a neighbour links into it, a ring's share, and its combos (Defense.ComboSlot: a copied shot takes no combo, his own neither).
  • Kills and XP of copied shots are credited to him, not the source.

Quirks

  • He never checks CanShoot, so he keeps firing during hold-fire and when cards would stop a tower shooting.
  • A copied beam tower (Old Varnish) fires without its wind-up, because Ready is not asked.
  • Copying a Moth Squadron lets out swarms owned by the Squadron itself: they count against its swarm cap and their later chews are credited to it.
  • Every copied shot also adds one to the source's shot count.
  • He swaps source the moment another neighbour has more kills.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Fire, IntervalOf, Launch, RangeOf
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/UnitCheck.cs, Tables/GameStore/GameStoreCheck.cs

MELTS ARMOUR

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 14 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On each shot he touches the one dude the engine aimed him at (Melee, Joe's outline, #251; until #251 everything in a 48-degree cone).
  • That dude first has its armour stripped for 4 s, then takes one Strike of the card damage (base 6) towards it.

Targeting

  • The engine's: the nearest dude he can see within his range (Melee).

Effects

  • Strips armour (StripArmour, the no-armour mark) for 4 s on every dude he touches; every tower's hits on it ignore armour meanwhile.
  • Fire damage type, with DotShare 0.2 in the base card: the hit sets the dude Burning by the shared rules (answer 8); the code sets nothing alight itself.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads f.CardDamage; his reach is the engine's (Range).
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats, Radius and Pierce.

Quirks

  • The strip comes before his own hit, so his own touch already ignores armour.
  • The counters Burned and Melted are static and never reset; no other code reads them.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Marked, Strike, StripArmour
  • Marks it puts on dudes: no-armour
  • Marks it reads: no-armour
  • Named outside its folder in: Tables/Workbench/WorkbenchCheck.cs

Tray blurb
Clamps one enemy and cranks as it walks on. One huge crush at the end, armour or not. Extremely unsympathetic.
Tray tag line
CRUSHES ONE ENEMY

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsMeleewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
KeywordsnoneJoe's unit list (#247)
Damage per hit40259.2 hp (x DamageScale 0.18 x Balance.Damage 36)
Fire rate0.25 /severy 4 s (0.25 /s), the mini's own interval
Range22 cm22 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 18 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • A shot clamps jaws on the target. They ride along with it for 2 s while it keeps walking.
  • Then armour and shield are stripped for 0.2 s, and the crush lands: one Strike for the card damage captured at clamp time, as an area hit.
  • If the dude in the jaws dies, to his crush or to anyone else, they snap shut at once (Joe, #251): shut in 0.08 s where it was, turning rust red, with a clack, a flash, a burst of grit and a ring on the ground; held shut 0.7 s, then gone (Snaps). Each plate is 0.6 cm thick, 2.6 x the dude's size tall and 2.2 x long.
  • A crush the dude survives lets the jaws go at once.

Targeting

  • Engine default, but a dude already in any Vice Admiral's jaws is passed over (SkipTarget, Joe, #251), so he aims at another and no reload is spent on it (Wasted counts any that still are).
  • Melee delivery: the engine never picks a floating dude.

Effects

  • At the crush it strips armour (no-armour) and shield (no-shield) for 0.2 s.
  • It marks a newly clamped dude vice-immune for 2 + 3 s. The mark now only decides whether the log and the Clamped counter call it held.
  • Nobody is held or slowed (issue 439).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads Damage (f.CardDamage, frozen when the jaws close). Range and FireRate come through the engine.
  • Repeats are ignored.
  • The crush goes through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply. It has no direction, so Force gives no knockback.

Quirks

  • If a clamped dude becomes floating before the crush, the Melee Strike does nothing.
  • Several Vice Admirals share one list of jaws, ticked once a frame.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire, SkipTarget
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, Strike, StripArmour, StripShield
  • Marks it puts on dudes: no-armour, no-shield, vice-immune
  • Marks it reads: vice-immune
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Tables/Workbench/WorkbenchCheck.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 24 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On fire it throws a disc in a straight line from the muzzle out to its reach at 55 cm/s, then the disc flies back to where it started.
  • It aims down the line with the most dudes on it: for every dude in reach that it can see (Direct, #251) it counts how many chests lie within 1.8 x size of the full-length line towards that dude, and takes the best.
  • Past the dude it was aimed at, the disc skims 0.6 cm over the ground rather than digging in; on the way out it turns for home early where scenery or a table blocker is in its path (Walled).
  • Every dude the disc passes within 1.8 x its size of is struck once on the way out and can be struck once more on the way back, each time for the full card damage.

Targeting

  • The engine's target (one it can see) only matters if no other dude it can see is in reach for the line count.
  • Lines are only aimed at dudes in sight (Direct, Joe's outline, #251); until #251 the disc flew through hills and scenery.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage per cut; reach is api.RangeOf with a 6 cm floor.
  • Repeats and Pierce are ignored: one disc per fire, and it always passes through everything.
  • Cuts go through Strike travelling along the disc's direction (reversed on the way back), so card on-hit effects and Force cards apply.

Quirks

  • Several discs can be in the air at once (a throw takes about 2 x reach / 55 s, less than the reload).
  • If the tower is lifted or sold while the disc is out, the disc vanishes once it turns round.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Tables/Workbench/WorkbenchCheck.cs

STEADIES + STAGGERS

Range16 cm16 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre (diagonals too).
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 16 cm, beside other towers.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Never attacks. Every 3 s (the mini's interval) it plays its pose, flashes each neighbour and shows its field as a ring; this changes nothing.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: no aim and no target.

Effects

  • Buff on the ring round it (the 6 touching hexes, or the 8 tiles round it on a square table; answer 21): +20% damage and +10% fire rate; no range since #251.
  • Stagger aura (Joe, #251): every frame each dude within its field (its range, 16 cm at base, measured flat; no line of sight needed) is staggered with the shared api.Stagger for the shared 0.5 s, when the shared rule allows: not while staggered, nor in the 2 s cool-off after (answer 12). A staggered dude stops and its shield drops (engine rules).
  • Non-damaging (answer 24): base damage 0, no damage card offered; it levels from the stagger's control time and the support share of the shots it buffs.
  • The on-slope bonus (x1.3 from every tower on a dude on a slope in reach) is gone (#251).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Range cards (api.RangeOf) widen the stagger field; Damage cards are never offered.

Quirks

  • The aura's damage bonus does not apply to splash hits (DamageAround), an engine rule.
  • The static counter Staggered counts from the player's start; only the check reads it.

  • Engine verbs its code calls: RangeOf, Stagger

EATS ANY BUT A BOSS

Range11.5 cm11.5 cm, the mini's own range
Force22 cm shove for a grunt from a hit that travels (scaled by weight), if its code strikes with travel
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Bot placement: reach 11.5 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Deadly (#247, Joe's answer 15): any target that is not a boss or a mini-boss is swallowed whole, whatever its hit points, armour or shield (DeadlyRule.CanDevour).
  • Swallow: the dude is held 0.6 s. After 0.22 s it is removed as a kill credited to the trap (api.Remove), and the trap gets XP for its current hp.
  • Swallow, then: the trap is busy digesting for min(12, 3 + 0.05 x armour + 0.005 x shield) seconds (DeadlyRule.DigestSecs; hit points do not count), plus 0.3 s.
  • Otherwise it bites: one Strike for the line's card damage, aimed from the muzzle (so the line's Force of 2 knocks it back).
  • While digesting it does nothing at all: Ready is false and Fire returns at once.

Targeting

  • Engine default. It takes whatever the engine picks and decides at fire time whether to swallow or bite, so it may bite a big dude even with small ones in reach.
  • Melee delivery: the engine never picks a floating dude for it.

Effects

  • A 0.6 s hold on a dude it swallows (glue rules apply).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • A bite reads Damage (f.CardDamage) and goes through Strike, so card on-hit effects and Force apply.
  • A swallow ignores the damage stat and bypasses Strike: no card on-hit hooks run, though the kill counts for the trap.
  • Repeats are ignored. Range and FireRate come through the engine.

Quirks

  • Answer 15 holds: hit points (late-wave growth too) never decide the swallow or the digestion; only boss or mini-boss rank stops it, and armour and shield lengthen the digestion.
  • If another tower kills the prey during the 0.22 s snap, the digestion is cut to at most 0.4 s.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Ready, Fire, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: After, Hold, Remove, Strike
  • Shared rules it reads: Balance.Damage
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/TestRange/RangeCheck.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Cone (#251, Joe's outline): each shot pours a wedge of water from the rose toward the target, the shared ConeRules cone (60 degrees, as long as its range). The pour does no damage.
  • Every dude in the wedge that terrain does not hide is soaked: mark wet for 5 s, which is the shared Wet status (answer 10).
  • Moss (Lingering, Slow): if the dude it aimed at is alive, a moss patch goes down on the road under it, the shared lingering patch (TowerApi.Linger): 3.6 cm across, 4 s (answer 22).
  • Every 0.5 s the patch hits each dude in it for 0.3 x the shot's card damage, and it slows whoever stands in it to 0.6 of its speed (the slow floors and the shrug-off rules apply).

Effects

  • Wet (the shared status): +25% from Cold and Electric hits and 25% less from Fire, from every tower.
  • The moss is drawn as its own little garden that grows in and dries up with the patch.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • The can itself is non-damaging (Joe); its damage lives in the moss, which reads the line's Damage, so damage cards feed the moss. The tower is therefore not flagged non-damaging (Peter's call on answer 24, for Joe to confirm).
  • Range is api.RangeOf (the wedge's length). The moss ticks go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply; as an area hit they take no aura damage bonus.
  • Repeats are ignored: one pour per shot.

  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • A beam tower: after a 0.7 s wind-up it fires one beam along the aim, out to its range or the first terrain in the way.
  • It looks along the beam in 0.5 cm steps (from 1.5 cm out to 2 cm before the end) for the first point inside a stripe of light.
  • No stripe on the way: one beam whose first 1 + Pierce dudes along it (within 1.1 x their size) each take 60% of the card damage.
  • A stripe on the way: the stretch up to it hits the same way at 60%, then the beam splits into three at 16 degrees apart.
  • Each of the three split beams runs on for the rest of the range (at least 8 cm) and hits the first dude on it not hit already, at 75% of the card damage.

Effects

  • The stripes: bands 9.5 cm wide every 24 cm, slanted at 20 degrees, fixed in table coordinates; they are drawn while a Sun Catcher is in play.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads f.CardDamage, Range (api.RangeOf) and Pierce (line.Stats[Stat.Pierce], more dudes on the unsplit stretch only).
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats: one beam per shot.

Quirks

  • No beam ever does full card damage: unsplit hits do 60% and split hits 75%. The summary says light does full damage.
  • Each split beam hits one dude at most, whatever the Pierce.
  • The beams pick dudes along the line with no sight check of their own; only the beam's end is cut by terrain.
  • The stripes are worked out from position alone, so they exist on every table, not only the sill.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotAdded, OnSlotRemoved, Update, Ready, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: AlongLine, RangeOf, Strike, Sweep
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On a shot, every dude within its range (flat, plus half its scale), bosses too, takes the full card damage through Strike, as an area hit.
  • Stagger (#251, Joe's outline): its line carries the Stagger tag, so the shared hit rules stagger each dude it hits (0.5 s, shield down, then a 2 s cool-off; a boss at most 0.5 s).
  • Debuff (armour removal, small units): before the hit, a small dude's armour comes off for 3 s (the shared StripArmour), so the ring itself goes through. Small means grunt tier, not a boss or a mini-boss; the armoured grunts (Mould Line Mike, a Resin Chunk) count, which is where the crack matters.

Targeting

  • Engine default; the target does not matter, the pulse hits every dude in range. No sight check for the dudes it hits.

Effects

  • The shared Stagger, and the shared armour strip (mark no-armour) on small dudes only.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads Damage (f.CardDamage) and Range (api.RangeOf for the hit radius). FireRate comes through the engine.
  • Repeats are ignored.
  • Hits go through Strike with no direction, so card on-hit effects apply but the Force of 3 gives no knockback.

Home table

  • On its home window sill, every gust of the sill wind (one every 11 s) makes each placed chime that can shoot ring once for free, at the line's full card damage, and say 'GUST'. Its reload is untouched.
  • A gust ring happens even when nobody is in reach, in which case it hits nothing.

CHEWS, THEN HOPS

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play

Range22 cm22 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 30 cm/s.
  • Bot placement: reach 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each shot lets out a swarm of moths at the engine's target, unless this tower already has 2 swarms out (then the shot does nothing).
  • The swarm flies at 32 cm/s, ignoring walls, to the target's chest.
  • Within 1.2 cm it chews: one Strike of the line's card damage every 0.5 s.
  • After 4 s on one dude, or when it dies, the swarm hops to the nearest living dude within 14 cm of the swarm, at most 3 times; then it goes home.
  • A swarm also goes home after 16 s in total, or when nobody is within 14 cm.

Targeting

  • Joe (#251): "Melee, Direct, Physical, Hop, Special(moths)". Direct: the first dude is the engine's target, which needs a clear line of sight from the lampshade.
  • Each hop goes to the nearest living dude to the swarm that the swarm can see from where it is (api.Blocked), ignoring bosses and range.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Chew damage is the line's Damage stat read when the swarm was let out, through Strike with no travel direction, so on-hit cards and DoT apply on every chew.
  • Repeats are ignored; FireRate only sets how often a new swarm can go out; Range only matters for the first target.

Quirks

  • Melee: a bite cannot reach a floating dude (the shared Melee rule in Strike).
  • Swarms are removed when the tower leaves the game.
  • Up to 1 + 3 dudes per swarm, and a long-lived dude is left after 4 s even if alive.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs

Fire rate0.5 /severy 2 s (0.5 /s), the mini's own interval
Range15 cm15 cm, the mini's own range
Radius16 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 14 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • When something is in range it flashes: every dude whose flat distance from the bulb is within the line's Radius (at least 6 cm) plus half its size is hit once.
  • Each hit is the full card damage through Strike as Arcane area damage (no direction, power 0.3).
  • Joe (#251): "Pulse, Arcane, Special(Fire). Base attack is Arcane, repeat hits set dudes on fire." Each dude the flash hits is marked bulb-lit for 3 s (a flash every 2 s at base, so a dude walking through is flashed two or three times).
  • A dude flashed again while marked, by any Bare Bulb, is set Burning through the shared Burning status (Walker.SetAlight): 5 stacks if it was not burning, 1 more if it was, the hold reset, credited to the bulb.
  • Its burn burns on that flash's hit on the dude, times the bulb's AuraBurn.

Targeting

  • The flash circle is centred on the bulb, not on the target; the target only has to be in Range for it to fire.
  • There is no sight check: walls and hills do not shield anyone inside the circle.

Effects

  • Burning from a repeat flash (Fire's secondary effect on an Arcane tower).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage via Strike, so on-hit card effects apply to every dude in the circle; the burn grows with the flash's damage.
  • Radius cards widen the circle (api.RadiusOf); Range cards only change when it fires; FireRate cards bring the repeat sooner.
  • Answer 3: no buffs on the pulse, but its ignite can be buffed. It is the one tower with AurasOnBurnOnly: the auras round it are collected, its damage, rate and range stay x1, and their damage buff goes into AuraBurn, the burn it sets.
  • Repeats are ignored: one flash per shot.

Quirks

  • It hits regardless of height, using flat distance only.
  • The bulb-lit mark is shared by every Bare Bulb: a dude flashed by one bulb and then by another catches fire.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, RadiusOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: bulb-lit
  • Marks it reads: bulb-lit
  • Shared rules it reads: HitRules.IgniteCap, HitRules.IgniteSecs
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CcWall.cs, Game/LobbedCardsCheck.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#251): "Beam, Chain, Fire, Special(light string beam)." Each shot strikes the target with the full card damage (the Beam, Direct).
  • Then the shared TowerApi.Chain jumps it to the nearest unhit living dude within 9 cm of the last one, as many jumps as the line's Bounces (4 at base, so five dudes), each 0.85 x the one before (85%).
  • Every link goes through Strike, in the direction it travelled, and every hit is Fire, so it burns through the shared Burning.

Targeting

  • Direct: the first dude needs a clear line of sight. Jumps ignore sight and the tower's range: only distance from the last dude counts.

Effects

  • The look: the links are drawn as a string of old-school C9 bulbs (BeamFx.LightString), big warm teardrops in five colours on a sagging dark-green wire, a bulb every 1.8 cm.
  • The string stays lit 0.32 s and fades over 0.12 s. The linger is drawing only: the damage is all at the moment of the shot.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage via f.CardDamage and Strike per link, so on-hit effects and the base burn (Fire damage, DotShare 0.4 in the stat block) apply to every link.
  • Bounces cards add jumps (CHAINS on the corkboard); the jump range and falloff are consts. Radius, Pierce and Repeats are not read by its code.

Quirks

  • A jump can go back towards the tower or to dudes far out of its range, as long as they are within 9 cm of the last one hit.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Chain, Strike

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Field: radius 18 cm (aura keystones read it).
  • Bot placement: reach 16 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.16 shots/s but the mini fires every 7 s (0.143/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#251): "Pulse, Arcane, Stagger, Debuff(damage taken). Implement 'brave' temporary immunity."
  • Each fire says boo in a ring round the Ghost itself (a Pulse): every living dude within the radius plus half its scale, on the flat. The radius is the line's Radius (18 cm at base, the same as its Range) times the support keystone field scale (Deep Mark, Wide Field).
  • Each dude in the ring is marked ghost-scared for 5 s and takes one Strike of the shot's card damage with no travel direction (power 0.3). The line carries Stagger, so the hit staggers it through the shared Stagger (0.5 s, shields down).
  • Brave: once the Ghost's stagger lands, the dude is marked ghost-brave for the stagger plus 6 s, and no Ghost's hit staggers it while it lasts (CanStagger). It is still haunted and still struck.

Targeting

  • It fires when a dude is in its Range; the ring is round the Ghost, not the target.

Effects

  • While a dude carries ghost-scared, every hit on it from any tower is x1.25 (+25%). Big and small dudes alike.
  • Stagger: the shared one; bosses and mini-bosses are stopped 0.5 s at most. Other towers' staggers on a brave dude keep the shared cool-off only.
  • As an aura keystone field: InField is the ghost-scared mark, FieldTakes is x1.25, field radius 18 cm round the Ghost.
  • ghost-scared is a decoy mark: the ghost earns decoy time while it lasts.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the shot's card damage (Damage stat) and the line's Radius stat (via RadiusOf, at least 6 cm); Repeats are ignored.
  • The boo goes through Strike, so card on-hit effects, the damage type and the Stagger apply.

Quirks

  • Until #251 the boo was a 10 cm ring laid round a target up to 18 cm away; it is now the Ghost's own 18 cm ring.
  • The x1.25 is applied once per ghost in play: with two ghosts placed, a scared dude takes x1.25 twice, whichever ghost scared it.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, DamageTaken, InField, FieldTakes, FieldReach, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, RadiusOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: ghost-brave, ghost-scared
  • Marks it reads: ghost-brave, ghost-scared
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Splash: one DamageAround of the card damage over that ring (the shared 85% and falloff).
  • Toss (#252, Joe's outline): then every dude in the ring that is still alive, not a boss or mini-boss and not tossed in the last 4 s, is thrown by the shared Toss (TowerApi.Toss): straight up, down where it went up, the fall taking 25% of its full health (answer 13). It is marked kicked meanwhile.
  • Bosses and mini-bosses take only the thud. Two rings flash on the beat (the second 0.12 s later at 70% of the radius).

Targeting

  • The engine target only decides when it fires; the beat goes to everyone in the ring.
  • Obstructing (answer 19): it may stand on the road itself; dudes walk through it and take the beat.

Effects

  • The shared Toss: the dude does not walk while it is up, and the fall is its own hit, credited to the drum.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Ring radius is api.RangeOf (the Range stat, auras and run multiplier); the Radius stat is not read.
  • Damage is f.CardDamage, dealt through DamageAround, so on-hit cards apply; the toss's fall is a share of the dude's health, not the card damage.
  • Repeats are ignored: one beat, one toss per dude.

Home table

  • On the garage, a drum on its home table listens to the garage drum machine: on each thump, every drum that is placed, can shoot, has a live target and is at least 75% reloaded fires at once.
  • If its next beat comes within 5 frames of that forced fire, it pops 'ON THE BEAT' over the drum.
  • When no drum is left in the garage it stops listening; the next drum placed listens again.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: After, CanShoot, DamageAround, Mark, Marked, OnHomeTable, RangeOf, Toss
  • Marks it puts on dudes: kicked
  • Marks it reads: kicked
  • Shared rules it reads: CardsConfig.LaunchSeconds
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Game/UnitCheck.cs

Radius2 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Stands on the road as well as on build tiles.
  • Bot placement: reach 13 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 13 cm; its mini reaches 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On the engine's fire, if no coil is out and it is not coiling, it loops a coil across the road (#252, Joe's outline).
  • Obstructing (answer 19): standing on a road spot, the coil lies across the road 3.5 cm upstream of its own spot (its base would hide a coil right under it), so dudes cross it before they reach the cable; from a build tile, 2.6 cm ahead of the target (at most 3 cm before the road's end). It lies on the road's walking surface, where the dude it aims at walks.
  • The coil works once it has landed (0.28 s): every dude on that route within 0.8 cm along the road of it, without the mark tangled, is shocked: one Strike of the line's card damage (base 15), a contact hit with no direction.
  • The hit is Electric and carries the Stagger tag, so the shared hit rules shock it (the Shock status and a jump to two more dudes, answer 5) and stagger it (0.5 s, shield down, then a 2 s cool-off).
  • The coil is spent after 3 dudes or 7 s; then it coils for 2 s before it can loop again.

Targeting

  • Ready is false while the coil is out or coiling.
  • Dudes with the mark tangled pass the coil untouched.

Effects

  • Mark tangled for 8 s, one of the engine's decoy marks, so it earns decoy time.
  • The coil is drawn as three thick loops of lead in an unlit live-wire orange-red with bright sparks on its live ends, wide across the road and past its edges, so it reads at the default camera at true size on the garage's cyan, yellow and grey roads.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads only the line's Damage (line.Stats[Stat.Damage]); the hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats, Radius (base 2) and Force; Range only matters for the engine's targeting.

Quirks

  • Bosses are shocked like anyone else (the stagger stops a boss for at most 0.5 s).
  • From a build tile the coil is not limited to its range: it lands 2.6 cm ahead of the target wherever that is.
  • Only dudes on the coil's own route count.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Ready, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: tangled
  • Marks it reads: tangled
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).

  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Never attacks. A spot of light sweeps to and fro along one road, 5.5 cm each side of a centre point, one there-and-back every 5.5 s.
  • On the mini's fire interval it plays a strobe pose; this changes nothing.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: no aim and no target.
  • The swept stretch is centred on the point of any route nearest the tower (kept 5.5 + 1 cm from the road's ends), chosen again only when the tower moves.

Effects

  • A dude within 3.2 cm (flat, plus 0.4 x its scale) of the spot's centre is lit: mark lit for 0.3 s, renewed every frame, and a ring at its feet.
  • Every hit from any tower other than a Stage Light on a lit dude is multiplied by 1.3 (+30%).
  • Two Stage Lights over the same dude count once: only the one with the lowest slot index gives the bonus.
  • It is an aura-keystone field: Deep Mark and Wide Field scale the spot's radius, Spotlight slows dudes in it, Lingering Mark keeps the x1.3 after they leave.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads no line stats: Range, Damage and FireRate cards change nothing; only keystones that scale fields change the spot.
  • Non-damaging (#252, answer 24; no damage type on Joe's line, so Physical rather than Arcane, with a Cone delivery): it is never offered a damage card.

Quirks

  • There is no range check: the spot lies on the nearest road however far away it is.
  • It stacks with other damage-taken towers (a Spirit Level, a Night Light) by multiplication; only other Stage Lights do not stack.
  • It shares the lit mark key with the Night Light.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, DamageTaken, InField, FieldTakes, FieldReach
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark
  • Marks it puts on dudes: lit
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/OverhaulLobbedCards.cs, Game/LobbedCardsCheck.cs

Attack

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 24 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 24 cm; its mini reaches 15 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#252): "Beam, Fire, Linger": a fire beam; dudes in the beam are affected; the beam falls to the ground and lingers, dudes stepping in it take fire damage.
  • Each fire is a beam at the target's chest height from 2.4 cm in front of the bottle along the flat direction to the target, past it by 5 cm (length clamped between 6 cm and range + 5 cm).
  • The first 8 dudes within 1.4 x their size of the beam take one Strike of the shot's card damage (Fire, power 0.3).
  • The beam falls to the table over 0.3 s, then lies there: the shared LingerLine along it for 4 s, 2.2 cm wide plus a margin, ticking 0.7 x the shot's card damage every 0.5 s on whoever stands in it.

Targeting

  • The fallen line lies where it fell, across any road; it burns any dude on it, from any route.

Effects

  • Every hit is Fire, so it sets dudes Burning by the shared rules. The line is drawn burning (the shared burning markers) over a red sauce line.
  • Footprints (a look only): a dude that walked through a line leaves red footprints (radius 0.95 cm, 3.5 s), up to 26 per bottle.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the shot's card damage for the beam and the fallen line, and Range via RangeOf for the length. Repeats are ignored. All hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.

Quirks

  • It slows nobody. Until #252 it was Physical and squeezed a line along the ground that ticked for 9 s.
  • A dude standing on two fallen lines takes both lines' ticks.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: After, AlongLine, LingerLine, Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: foot-cool, ketchup
  • Marks it reads: foot-cool, ketchup
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Field: radius 19 cm (aura keystones read it).
  • Bot placement: reach 24 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 1 shots/s but the mini fires every 0.6 s (1.67/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 24 cm; its mini reaches 19 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#252): "Aura, Passive, Debuff(damage taken), Special(Annoys - dudes stop, turn to face the Gnome)". It never fires or deals damage (non-damaging, answer 24).
  • Every frame it takes on each new small dude within reach (any side, on the flat): marks it gnome-busy for 3.5 s and gnome-bored for 3.5 + 5 s, and turns it to face the gnome (TowerApi.Face) for 3.5 s.
  • While gnome-busy the dude stands still: the gnome's SpeedFactor is 0 for it, a stop the slow floor does not lift.
  • The reach is 19 cm times the support keystone field scale (Deep Mark, Wide Field). Every 0.55 s while anyone is busy the gnome wobbles and the busy dudes flinch.

Targeting

  • Self-driven. At most 5 busy dudes at a time, counting every dude marked gnome-busy within reach + 2 cm, whichever gnome marked it.
  • It skips bosses and mini-bosses (they walk past, never stopped or turned), dudes already busy, and dudes still gnome-bored. No sight check.

Effects

  • Every hit from any tower on a dude marked gnome-busy is x1.35 (+35%).
  • As an aura keystone field: InField is the gnome-busy mark, FieldTakes is x1.35, field radius 19 cm.
  • gnome-busy is a decoy mark: the gnome earns decoy time while it lasts.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Its code reads nothing from its attack line, so cards that change it do nothing; only the support keystones' field scale changes its reach. No damage cards are offered (non-damaging).

Quirks

  • The x1.35 is applied once per gnome in play: with two gnomes placed, a busy dude takes x1.35 twice, wherever it is.
  • A lane is never held for ever: one dude is stopped at most 3.5 s in every 3.5 + 5 s.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, SpeedFactor, DamageTaken, InField, FieldTakes
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Face, Mark, Marked
  • Marks it puts on dudes: gnome-bored, gnome-busy
  • Marks it reads: gnome-bored, gnome-busy
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CcWall.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Stands on the road as well as on build tiles.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Never aims. Its pencil tip sweeps a circle round its spot, one turn every 2.6 s; the radius is the line's range (api.RangeOf, at least 3 cm; base 9 cm). The circle it draws is only a drawing.
  • The tip is the blade (Joe, #252): each frame, every dude within 1.2 cm plus half its size of the arc the tip swept is struck with the line's Damage stat through Strike (base 9), in its walking direction. Until #252 the drawn line cut whoever crossed it.
  • The same dude cannot be cut again by the same compass for 0.4 s (mark cut plus the slot index).

Targeting

  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it.
  • Obstructing (answer 19): it may stand on the road, and sweeps round its spot there; Mobile is the sweep (answer 18), not a chase.

Effects

  • Each cut leaves a pencil tick on the road for 20 s (at most 36 ticks kept), and marks the dude pencilled for 4 s; nothing reads that mark.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Range cards widen the circle (api.RangeOf); Damage cards change the cut (line.Stats[Damage]); every cut goes through Strike, so on-hit effects apply.
  • The drawing speed scales with the fire-rate aura (at least 0.5 x) but not with FireRate cards. Repeats and Radius are not read.

Quirks

  • Its line is Melee, so Strike skips floating dudes: they cross the line uncut.
  • It keeps cutting while the towers are held from firing.
  • Lifting it resets the drawing (only the picture: the tip cuts from its first turn).

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: cut, pencilled
  • Marks it reads: cut
  • Named outside its folder in: Tables/Schooldesk/SchoolDeskCheck.cs, Tables/TreehouseMap/TreehouseMapBoard.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Stands on the road as well as on build tiles.
  • Bot placement: reach 8 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.154 shots/s but the mini fires every 5 s (0.2/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 8 cm; its mini reaches 14 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Self-driven. When placed it stretches a band from its hook across the nearest road point (at least 2.5 cm from the tower) to a pin 3.2 cm past it; it has no band if that point is out of its range. Standing on a road spot (Obstructing, answer 19) the band spans the road at his own spot, a pin 3.2 cm out on either side.
  • While taut, every dude whose flat distance to the band is under 0.95 + 0.2 x its size is on it, and held (below).
  • It flicks when someone leans and either the lean has lasted 1 s, or the leaners plus the dudes in the 9 cm of road behind the band weigh 3 or more (a heavy dude counts 2) and the lean has lasted 0.3 s.
  • A flick strikes every dude on that road from 9 cm behind the band to 1.2 cm past it, plus all leaners, for the line's card damage, the hit travelling back up the road.
  • Each survivor is then pushed back 80 push units (about 11 cm of road before diminishing returns) and staggered for 0.6 s.
  • After a flick the band hangs slack for 5.5 s, then is taut again.

Targeting

  • Never uses the engine's targeting; the band's place decides everything.
  • Leaners are found by distance to the band only, on any route; the flick zone is on the band's own route.
  • Heavy means a boss, any non-grunt tier, or an enemy with 600 or more hp or scale 1.3 or more.

Effects

  • Hold (Joe, #252; #471): a dude on the taut band stops dead, a boss too: its SpeedFactor is exactly 0, the stop the slow floor does not lift (#476). Nobody is held longer than 1 s, because the flick comes then. Until #252 they leaned at 0.3 of their speed (a boss 0.7).
  • Push of 80 units (Push divides by 7, so about 11 cm, cut by the engine's diminishing shoves) and a 0.6 s stagger on every flicked dude.
  • Shove: the flick is the shared Push, always back down the road the dude came (answer 14). Its line is Melee (Joe; it was Pulse and Knockback until #252).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Smack damage reads the line's Damage stat (6 on the base card); reach uses api.RangeOf, so range cards let it span a road further away.
  • The fire rate stat is ignored: the cycle is fixed by 1 and 5.5.
  • Repeats and Radius are ignored.
  • The smack goes through Strike, so card on-hit effects and Force apply.

Quirks

  • A lone dude is flicked too, once it has leaned 1 s.
  • It keeps working while the towers' fire is held: it never asks whether it may shoot.
  • Re-aims the band only when the tower moves more than 1 cm.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, SpeedFactor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Push, RangeOf, Stagger, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/TestRange/RangeCheck.cs, Tables/Schooldesk/SchoolDeskCheck.cs

gluestick, pritt
Tray blurb
Swipes a stripe of glue across the road. Whoever walks through it sticks for a moment. Hurts nobody.
Tray tag line
GLUES THE ROAD

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typeGlueno damage: it only glues
Delivery tagsSplashwhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
KeywordsLingering, ObstructingJoe's unit list (#247)
Damage per hit0none
Fire rate0.222 /severy 2.4 s (0.417 /s), the mini's own interval
Range16 cm16 cm, the mini's own range
Radius3 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Stands on the road as well as on build tiles.
  • Bot placement: reach 11 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.222 shots/s but the mini fires every 2.4 s (0.417/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 11 cm; its mini reaches 16 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each fire swipes a stripe of glue across the target's route 3.5 cm ahead of the lead dude, at most 3 cm before the route's end; if that point is out of reach, 1.5 cm ahead instead. Standing on a road spot (Obstructing, answer 19) it glues the road at its own spot.
  • The stripe is the shared glue pad (GlueFx.Splash, Brother Superglue's glue) of the line's Radius (base 3 cm): it holds whoever walks through it hard for 2.2 s, then lets go over 1.8 s (Linger: answer 22's 4 s).
  • Splash: everyone under the stripe takes the line's hit through DamageAround, for no damage (a Glue line), so its cards' statuses land.
  • After a swipe it winds for 4.5 s and cannot fire meanwhile.

Targeting

  • The engine picks the target; the code then takes the lead dude, the living dude furthest along the target's route within range of the tower (no sight check).

Effects

  • Glue: the pad's slow is the engine's (GlueFx.StickAt, read by Defense), with the engine's glue rules (the grip lets go after a while and leaves the dude slippery a moment; bosses resist).
  • Non-damaging (Joe: "No damage"; answer 24): Glue damage type with no base damage, no damage card offered; it earns control time from whoever its pad holds (NoteZone). Until #252 it held the first four over its line 1.6 s each and struck them for 1.5.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Radius cards widen the stripe; Range (RangeOf) is the reach for the lead dude. Damage is never read (a Glue line has none).
  • Its hit goes through DamageAround, so card on-hit statuses apply to everyone under the stripe.

Quirks

  • The stripe glues every dude under it, whichever route it walks.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Ready, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround, NoteZone, RangeOf
  • Named outside its folder in: Tables/Schooldesk/SchoolDeskCheck.cs

Card unitsIn play

Fire rate0.167 /severy 6 s (0.167 /s), the mini's own interval
Range6 cm6 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre.
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 4 cm, beside other towers.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Never shoots. It shows a digit from 1 to 9 and changes it every 6 s to a new digit, never the same twice in a row.
  • The screen flickers for the last 1.3 s before each change.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it.

Effects

  • Aura (Adjacent, answer 21): each tower on the ring round it (the 6 touching hexes, or the 8 tiles on a square table; AuraReach 1.5) gets damage + digit x 0.04 (+4% per point, 4 to 36%).
  • Non-damaging (Joe, #252; answer 24): Physical with no damage of its own (it was Arcane until #252), no damage card offered; it levels from the support share of the shots it boosts.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Its code reads no line stats: the Period (6) s clock is a const, so FireRate cards and auras do not speed it up.

Quirks

  • The first change comes 3.6 to 6 s after it is first placed (a random head start of up to 0.4 x 6); the clock runs only while placed.
  • The digits come from a per-tower seeded random sequence (seeded by slot index).
  • An aura damage bonus does not apply to splash hits made with DamageAround, but it does apply to the Cannon's blast, which strikes directly.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, AuraFor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: After
  • Named outside its folder in: Tables/Schooldesk/SchoolDeskCheck.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#253): "Wet, Glue, Lingering, Splash, Special(wasps, Blight). No base damage, wasps called inflict damage, stack Blight on targets."
  • Each fire lobs a squirt to the road 5 cm ahead of the target. The squirt hits nobody (no base damage).
  • Where it lands (a Splash of 3.3 cm): the shared glue pad (GlueFx: it holds 2.2 s and lets go over 1.8 s) and the shared Linger for 4 s, marking Wet (5 s) on whoever stands in it. The box keeps at most 3 puddles drawn.
  • Three wasps live round the box. While it has a puddle, each picks the slowest dude standing in one and stings it every 0.9 s: a Strike of the box's own line, its Damage stat, a Blight hit, so each sting stacks Blight.

Targeting

  • The engine aims the squirt; the wasps choose the most held or slowed dude in a puddle, then the one furthest back.

Effects

  • Glue (the shared pad's slow and grip), Wet (5 s after it walks out), Blight from the stings.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • The stings are the box's line, so damage cards raise them and status cards ride them (Peter's call, #253: the box is not a non-damaging unit, like the Watering Can's moss).
  • Radius and the puddle's life are consts; FireRate cards bring puddles sooner.

Quirks

  • Until #253 the squirt hit for 3, the puddle slowed to 55% for 11 s on its own, and the wasps' chews were Physical.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Fire, OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: IsHeld, Launch, Linger, NoteZone, Strike
  • Shared rules it reads: HitRules.SoakSecs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Joe (#253): "Melee, Physical, Hold, Debuff(damage taken)". Each fire clicks shut on the leader: the dude furthest along the road within reach and in sight, not just let go.
  • The click is a Strike of the line (Melee, Physical), then the shared Hold: the dude stops dead (#471) for 2 s, or 1.3 s for a boss or mini-boss (bosses are held too since #253).
  • A belt is drawn round its waist while it is buckled; the car's brake warning lets every buckled dude go 2.6 s later.

Targeting

  • Skips a dude buckled or let go in the last 4 s, and one the Air Freshener has turned (the shared carseat-control mark).

Effects

  • Debuff: a buckled dude takes x1.25 (+25%) from every hit; two buckles do not stack.
  • The shared no-re-hold rule applies (1.8 s held in all, then 4 s).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • The click reads the shot's card damage through Strike, so damage and on-hit cards apply; Range cards widen the leader search.

Quirks

  • Until #253 the click also staggered (not on Joe's line) and a boss only stumbled.

  • It bursts: the shared DamageAround over 3.8 cm (Blight), then the shared Linger cloud for 4 s, wandering about 1 cm, ticking 0.5 x the shot's card damage every 0.5 s.
  • Every dude that walks into the cloud, and is not nose-blind, is turned round: the Nightcap's numbers (answer 25): it walks back the way it came at x-0.5 for 5 s; a boss or mini-boss stops instead (factor 0). Then it is nose-blind for 4 s.
  • The tower keeps at most 2 clouds.

Targeting

  • Lobbed: it can throw over walls. A dude the Seat-Belt Buckle has held is out of reach (the shared carseat-control mark).

Effects

  • Blight from the burst and every tick; turned round or stopped while it smells the cloud.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • The burst and the ticks read the shot's card damage, so damage and Blight cards apply.

Quirks

  • Until #253 it was Arcane, its cloud drifted back along the road for 5.5 s, a grunt turned for 1.5 s and a boss only slowed.

  • Bot placement: reach 18 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.3 shots/s but the mini fires every 7 s (0.143/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Range mismatch: the card base says 9 cm but the mini's range is 24 cm. The game uses the mini's; the card number only scales it.

Attack

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Beam (#253, Joe's outline): on fire it draws an opaque, unlit red beam from the remote to the target that thins away over 0.45 s, landing at once (no wind-up); an additive line came out white on the couch's pale road.
  • Splash: one DamageAround of the card damage (Arcane) over the line's radius round the target's feet (the shared 85% and falloff).
  • Turned around (answer 25): every dude in the splash not marked channel-bored is marked channel for 5 s and channel-bored for 5 + 4.5 s.

Targeting

  • No sight check for the dudes round the target; only the engine's target needs to be seen.

Effects

  • While channel is on, a grunt marches back the way it came at -0.8 x its speed (a negative tower speed factor, the Nightcap's way); a boss or mini-boss stands still (factor 0, no floor).
  • channel-bored keeps a dude from being turned again until it wears off: the cooldown of answer 25.
  • No longer a debuff (Joe): it adds nothing to damage taken. channel is a decoy mark, so the tower earns decoy time for it.
  • A controlled dude wears an unlit red ring at its feet for as long as it is controlled, so a dude marching the wrong way reads at the default camera.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage through DamageAround; the splash radius is api.RadiusOf with a 3 cm floor (4.5 cm on the base card), so Radius cards widen it.
  • Its delivery is Beam with Splash. Repeats are ignored.

Quirks

  • The shared shove budget limits how much road a marching dude can lose.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On fire it lobs 5 kernels plus one per Repeat (at most the shared per-tower mine cap), each at a random point within 3.6 cm of where the target will be after that kernel's flight.
  • Each kernel's flight time is random between 0.4 and 0.65 s.
  • Mine (#253, Joe's outline): where a kernel lands it lies as a shared mine (TowerApi.LayMine) with a fuse: it pops on its own 0.8 s after landing (answer 22), stepped on or not.
  • The pop is the line's Fire splash over 2 cm round the kernel (the shared mine's DamageAroundChain), drawn as a flash, an orange ring bursting out, and a buttery popped kernel (1.8 x, about 3.5 cm) that springs up and shrinks away.

Targeting

  • Lobs, so sight follows the cannon's arc rules.
  • The scatter point is weighted to the middle (random radius times 3.6), and it leads the target by its current speed.

Effects

  • Fire: each pop burns, through the shared hit rules.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Every kernel's pop carries the full card damage; Repeats add one kernel each.
  • The pop's radius is the fixed 2 cm; the line's Radius stat is ignored.
  • Its delivery is Projectile, Mine and Splash.

Quirks

  • Kernels are random: one aimed at a lone dude can miss it entirely.
  • Two kernels popping close together can both hit the same dude.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire, OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Launch, LayMine

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Projectile (default Fire): a bolt at 120 cm/s, hit radius 1.35 cm round the chest, pierces with the Pierce stat (-15% a pass).
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 120 cm/s.
  • Bot placement: reach 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Flicks a coin at 120 cm/s aimed ahead of the target, with a random jitter of up to 1.1 cm sideways and 0.5 cm up.
  • On a hit it does one Strike of the coin's card damage.
  • Then the coin ricochets once: it turns towards the nearest other living dude within 9 cm of the one it hit that terrain does not hide, and flies straight at it.
  • The ricochet hit takes 15% less (the engine's pierce loss) and does one more Strike; after that the coin is spent.

Targeting

  • The ricochet target is the nearest dude to the first one hit, not a boss or the engine's choice.

Effects

  • Kills by this line pay a bounty of x1.5 (rounded), from the card base's LootBonus of 0.5.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is the projectile's card damage, through Strike, so on-hit cards apply to both hits.
  • Repeats give extra coins with a wider random spread (the default Fire).
  • Pierce above 1 lets the coin carry on in a straight line through more dudes after the ricochet, each pass 15% weaker; it never bounces twice.
  • LootBonus cards raise the kill bounty up to the 1.0 cap.

Quirks

  • The ricochet flies straight at where the second dude was, without homing, so it can miss a moving dude.
  • With nobody within 9 cm the coin stops at the first hit.
  • The summary comment says it is the only tower that pays for its own kills; the loot bonus is an ordinary stat that a card in the painters pack also gives.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: LaunchVelocity, OnProjectileHit, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs

  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Default lob: one cushion per shot (plus one per card Repeat, scattered) on a cannon arc, aimed by the engine at 55 cm/s.
  • Splash (#253, Joe's outline): where the cushion lands, one DamageAround of the card damage over the line's Radius (at least 2.5 cm, base 4), the shared 85% and falloff.
  • Linger, Slow: then a cloud of feathers lies there, the shared lingering patch (1.1 x the radius, 4 s, answer 22), slowing whoever walks in it to 0.6 of its speed; no damage of its own (answer 26).

Effects

  • The feather patch's slow, under the shared slow floors and shrug-off rules.
  • The cloud is drawn as two dozen unlit white feathers and a few teal tufts over a patch of unlit dark teal on the ground, so it reads at the default camera on the couch's pale road.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads Damage (the splash), Radius (through api.RadiusOf, minimum 2.5 cm), Range and FireRate through the engine, and Repeats.
  • The splash goes through DamageAround, so card on-hit effects apply; the hits have no direction, so the Force of 2 gives no knockback.

Quirks

  • A lob only lands on terrain (HitRadius 0), so a cushion never hits a dude in the air on the way down.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround, Linger, RadiusOf
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

Conker #

T-060 · conker · treehouse · 110 points

Home
The Treehouse (in that table's tray)

T-060, TowerKind 23

110 points
Affinity
Springy
Role
crowd control (its card pool)
Tray blurb
A conker on a string, swinging over the road. Whoever it catches is knocked back down the road.
Tray tag line
KNOCKS THEM BACK

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsMeleewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
KeywordsShoveJoe's unit list (#247)
Damage per hit721 hp (x DamageScale 1/12 x Balance.Damage 36)
Fire rate0.4 /severy 2.4 s (0.417 /s), the mini's own interval
Range8 cm10.5 cm, the mini's own range
Force33 cm shove for a grunt from a hit that travels (scaled by weight), if its code strikes with travel
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 8 cm.
  • Range mismatch: the card base says 8 cm but the mini's range is 10.5 cm. The game uses the mini's; the card number only scales it.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • It never fires: every frame the conker swings as a pendulum (2.6 s a full swing, up to 0.95 rad either side on a 4.1 cm string) from the tip of a 5.6 cm arm.
  • A living dude is hit when the ball is within 1.5 cm plus half the dude's size of it on the flat, and the ball is between 0.5 cm below its feet and 3.6 x its size above them.
  • A hit needs the ball to be moving at at least 30% of its top speed (so nothing is hit near the ends of the swing).
  • Each hit makes one Strike of the line's card damage back down the road (power 0.6), then shoves the dude back down the road it came with the shared Push (Joe, #253: Shove, always backward, answer 14). Until #253 it knocked dudes sideways across the road and staggered them.
  • Each hit counts as a shot and plays the mini's fire animation.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: it never aims at a dude. Every 1.2 s it looks for the nearest point of any road within 14 cm (sampled every 2 cm) and turns its arm towards it.
  • The swing runs along the arm's direction, towards and away from the tower, so it crosses the road when the arm points straight at it.
  • It hits whatever the ball touches, on any route, with no sight check; flock patches do not hide dudes from it.
  • A dude marked conkered is skipped; the mark lasts 2.2 s after each hit.

Effects

  • Shove: 5 cm of road back for a grunt (0.4 x that for a big dude: boss or any non-grunt tier), through the shared shove's diminishing returns and route-loss cap; a Slick dude (the Water Balloon) slides x1.5.
  • Marks sprung for 1.5 s on every dude it hits.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • It reads only the line's Damage stat (passed straight to Strike); Range, FireRate, Repeats and Radius are never read, so cards that change them do nothing to it.
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects, the damage type and the line's Force knockback (along the ball's velocity) apply.
  • It never asks CanShoot, so the holdfire switch and cards that stop a tower firing do not stop the swing.

Quirks

  • Its sprung mark is the same key the Spring Sergeant uses, so the Sergeant will not toss a dude the conker hit in the last 1.5 s (it looks for another dude).
  • If the ball is moving faster than 300 cm/s on the flat (the god-hand carrying it), nothing is hit.
  • It hits nothing while held in the hand.
  • With no road within 14 cm the arm does not turn, but the ball still swings and hits any dude that walks under it.

Home table

  • After a hit, a dude that is still alive and stands within 1.2 cm of a plank gap along its road is sent down it (SendDown). Only tables with holes have gaps (the treehouse, its home table); big walkers never go down.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, Push, SendDown, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: conkered, sprung
  • Marks it reads: conkered
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/TestRange/RangeCheck.cs, Tables/Treehouse/TreehouseCheck.cs, Tables/Treehouse/TreehouseTable.cs

LIGHTS UP ENEMIES

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Range20 cm22 cm, the mini's own range
Radius20 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Field: radius 20 cm (aura keystones read it).
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 16 cm.
  • Range mismatch: the card base says 20 cm but the mini's range is 22 cm. The game uses the mini's; the card number only scales it.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Runs itself in Update. It never shoots and does no damage: non-damaging (Joe, #253; answer 24), a Physical Cone (it was an Arcane Beam and Pulse until #253), no damage card offered; it levels from the support share of the shots it makes count.
  • Once a second it finds the road points (every 2 cm) within 0.8 x its reach. It aims the beam's centre at their average direction, and the beam is off when there are none.
  • It sweeps ±32 degrees round that direction, a full sweep every 5.5 s.
  • Every frame, every dude within 20 cm of the lens (flat, plus half its size) and inside the 28 degree cone of the beam's real heading gets lit.
  • Every 4.5 s it flares (the first one 1.5 s after it is placed): a ring and a flash. A lit dude stays lit 2.5 s after the sweep passes it (Peter's call, #253: as long as its moths stay, so what is seen is what it does; 0.35 s until #253), the same on a flare (2.5 s).

Targeting

  • No target: it lights whoever stands in the cone.
  • No sight check: walls and terrain do not block the light.

Effects

  • It lays the mark torch-lit (2.5 s, refreshed every frame in the beam; 2.5 s on a pulse) and the plain mark lit for the same time.
  • While torch-lit is on a dude, every hit on it from a tower other than a Torch does +25% (DamageTaken).
  • Field keystones: InField is the torch-lit mark. Deep Mark and Wide Field scale the reach through SupportKeystoneRules.FieldScale.
  • Special(moths) (Joe, #253, Peter's reading): the moths are the visible mark of the debuff. 3 glowing moths (3.5 x the model, unlit cream) circle every lit dude while it is lit, up to 12; with nobody lit, 3 hover at the lens. They do nothing else.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads no stat from its line. Its reach, cone, sweep and pulse are constants, so Damage, Range, FireRate, Radius, range auras and rate auras change nothing. Only the field keystones scale the reach.

Quirks

  • The bonus stacks between Torches. Every placed Torch multiplies a lit dude's damage by 1.25 (the mark is shared), so two Torches give x1.5625.
  • The bonus applies to hits from any tower, splash hits included.
  • unclear from code: nothing in Assets/Greytide reads the plain lit mark (the Night Light and Stage Light lay it too).

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, DamageTaken, InField, FieldTakes
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked
  • Marks it puts on dudes: lit, torch-lit
  • Marks it reads: torch-lit
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/OverhaulLobbedCards.cs, Game/LobbedCardsCheck.cs, Tables/Treehouse/TreehouseCheck.cs

  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
